18 14:25:55       In any event, some repainting and decoration will be

19 14:25:59   needed.

20 14:26:00       The main points of disagreement are really who was

21 14:26:02   responsible for the design of the basement

22 14:26:04   waterproofing, whether the resin injection works are now

23 14:26:07   complete and whether further injections are likely to be

24 14:26:09   needed, and also whether there is an ability to

25 14:26:12   repeatedly reinject using the cast in hoses or whether


                                      99
 1 14:26:16   they become exhausted.

 2 14:26:18       If we go to G3.9-6, please.

 3 14:26:22       So I was going to take you through some sort of

 4 14:26:25   general design principles of underground structures.

 5 14:26:28   I'll try and be as quick as possible.

 6 14:26:32       The main points I would like to make is, in this

 7 14:26:34   part of the world in particular, you normally go for

 8 14:26:36   a belt-and-braces approach, which is to provide two

 9 14:26:39   lines of defence.  This is because it's unreasonable to

10 14:26:41   expect the waterproof membrane, particularly in a large

11 14:26:45   structure such as this, to be entirely defect-free,

12 14:26:48   despite the best will in the world.  As you can imagine,

13 14:26:51   even a teeny sort of pinprick-size puncture will render

14 14:26:56   the membrane ineffective.

15 14:26:58       Also, what needs to be considered is that the

16 14:27:00   construction joints are inherent lines of weaknesses in

17 14:27:04   concrete structures that are susceptible to water

18 14:27:07   ingress, but they are nonetheless unavoidable because

19 14:27:11   you cannot cast the concrete for a large structure like

20 14:27:13   this in one go.  So you have to provide additional

21 14:27:16   waterproofing meshes at construction joints and these

22 14:27:18   are usually water bars or hydrophilic strips and resin

23 14:27:22   injection hoses.

24 14:27:24       Could we go to G3.9-7.

25 14:27:28       The basement for this development contained an


                                     100
 1 14:27:31   external unbonded membrane, and because it's unbonded,

 2 14:27:36   it would normally mean that any puncture in the membrane

 3 14:27:39   would essentially render it completely ineffective.  To

 4 14:27:43   get around this, it was compartmentalised by providing

 5 14:27:47   a grid of water bars that were heat-welded to the

 6 14:27:49   membrane, and then when you cast the concrete against

 7 14:27:52   the membrane, the water bars become embedded within the

 8 14:27:55   concrete, forming the apartments.

 9 14:27:58       Each compartment was provided with a number of resin

10 14:28:01   injection flanges which are essentially PVC valves that

11 14:28:04   are cast into the concrete and located on the outer

12 14:28:07   surface.  These valves are connected to resin injection

13 14:28:10   hoses that are cast into the slabs and the walls, and

14 14:28:13   these hoses terminate at injection boxes, which on this

15 14:28:17   job were located on the columns along the walls.

16 14:28:20       Slide G3.9-8, please.

17 14:28:24       So this is simply showing -- these are some images

18 14:28:28   of the compartmentalisation of the membrane, and the

19 14:28:33   bottom right-hand corner there shows what happens at

20 14:28:36   a construction joint.  There's a water bar at the bottom

21 14:28:39   and a resin injection hose midway through the thickness

22 14:28:43   of the slab.

23 14:28:44       Slide G3.9-9.

25 14:28:52   the water ingress now appears to have been largely

                                     102
 1 14:30:11       I should also note that design responsibility, as

 2 14:30:13   far as this pleaded issue is concerned, is not relevant

 3 14:30:15   because I don't consider there to be any problems with

 4 14:30:17   the design insofar as the water ingress from the resin

 5 14:30:20   injection boxes are concerned.

 6 14:30:23       Slide G3.9-11.

 7 14:30:28       So the next point of disagreement is whether the

 8 14:30:30   resin injection works are complete.

 9 14:30:32       To me, it appears that the water ingress into the

10 14:30:35   basement has been largely rectified.  I can't say

11 14:30:37   whether it has fully rectified the issue, because there

12 14:30:41   were elevated moisture readings and water staining

13 14:30:43   around some of the resin injection boxes.  This could be

14 14:30:46   due to ongoing water ingress or it could also be due to

15 14:30:49   the presence of hygroscopic salts, which have not been

16 14:30:54   fully cleaned and removed from the previous water

17 14:30:56   ingress.  These salts can absorb moisture from the

18 14:30:57   surrounding environment and therefore give the elevated

19 14:31:00   moisture readings and also cause further staining, and

20 14:31:04   also they can sometimes give spurious readings from the

21 14:31:06   moisture meter because the way moisture meters work is

22 14:31:10   they measure an electrical current between two probes,

23 14:31:13   and when you have water which is conducive, you get

24 14:31:18   a stronger current, and the salts themselves are also

25 14:31:21   conducive, so you sometimes get the same effect.


                                     103
 1 14:31:26       So to assess whether there is an ongoing issue,

 2 14:31:29   I think the water -- the moisture levels and the water

 3 14:31:32   ingress would need to be monitored over time and

 4 14:31:36   addressed through further resin injections as may be

 5 14:31:39   necessary.

 6 14:31:41       Repeated injections to resolve any water ingress in

 7 14:31:44   my experience is not uncommon, because basement

 8 14:31:48   structures with high water tables are notoriously

 9 14:31:51   difficult to waterproof in the first pass.

10 14:31:55       Slide G3.9-12.

                                     105
 1 14:34:06   and in my experiences, these hoses can be repeated used

 2 14:34:10   to inject resins, provided of course that the hoses are

 3 14:34:13   flushed out after each use and the right type of resin

 4 14:34:17   is used.

 5 14:34:18       I've looked at the product data sheets for the

 6 14:34:20   particular hoses used on this project and they confirm

 7 14:34:23   that they are reinjectable.  As far as I'm aware, it has

 8 14:34:29   not been pleaded that any of these cast in hoses cannot

 9 14:34:31   be reused, but if they can't be reused for whatever

10 14:34:35   reason, then the alternative option is to use what's

11 14:34:38   called drilled-in packers.  They're basically metal

12 14:34:42   nozzles which you've drilled into the concrete,

13 14:34:43   connected to the resin injection hoses, and then you

17 14:52:30       Slab G3.9-37, please.

18 14:52:33       So I'm now going to talk about the cause of the

19 14:52:36   water ingress -- and I should say the potential causes

20 14:52:38   of the water ingress.

21 14:52:39       In principle, the water ingress could be due to

22 14:52:41   design and/or workmanship issues.  There may well be

23 14:52:45   workmanship issues, but the as-built details are covered

24 14:52:48   by finishes, soil, et cetera, so the nature and extent

25 14:52:50   of any workmanship issues are unknown at present.


                                     119
 1 14:52:54       Based on my review of the documents, I consider that

 2 14:52:58   there are several design issues that could have caused

 3 14:53:00   or contributed to the water ingress.

 4 14:53:02       So the first one is the spacing of the expansion

 5 14:53:05   joints.  In my view, the expansion joints on the podium

 6 14:53:10   are spaced further apart than they normally would be for

 7 14:53:13   concrete structures.  For concrete structures they are

 8 14:53:16   typically between 30 to 50 metres apart.  On this

 9 14:53:19   podium, they are sort of 65 to 80 metres apart.  What

10 14:53:23   than means is that at the expansion joints you're going

11 14:53:26   to get a greater degree of movement due to thermal

12 14:53:29   effects and wind loads and things like that.

13 14:53:32       The next issue is that the expansion joint profiles

14 14:53:37   and the sealants that they used, which were approved by

15 14:53:40   the engineer, would not be able to accommodate the

16 14:53:44   likely movements at the expansion joints.  As

17 14:53:46   I mentioned earlier, the expansion joint profiles based

18 14:53:49   on the data sheet could only accommodate movements of

19 14:53:52   plus or minus 10 millimetres, and the sealants, based on

20 14:53:54   their data sheets, can only accommodate plus or minus

21 14:53:57   7.5 millimetres.  What this means is that if your

22 14:54:00   movements exceed your limits, you're likely to get the

23 14:54:02   sealants and the gaskets cracking.

24 14:54:06       The next main issue with the design, as far as I'm

25 14:54:09   concerned, is the lack of drainage to the secondary


                                     120
 1 14:54:12   podium slab.

 2 14:54:14       If you recall from the images that I put up on

 3 14:54:17   screen earlier, the waterproof membrane is applied to

 4 14:54:19   this slab, and because there isn't any lack of drainage,

 5 14:54:23   what that means is that any water that gets in through

 6 14:54:26   the floor finishes will sit on top of that membrane and

 7 14:54:29   it's got nowhere to go, unless there's a release point

 8 14:54:31   in the slab itself or through the expansion joints.

 9 14:54:37       The secondary podium slab is also lightly

10 14:54:40   reinforced, which means that it will crack due to

11 14:54:43   shrinkage and thermal effects, and it's unclear to me at

12 14:54:46   this point whether the waterproof membrane that was

13 14:54:48   specified and used could accommodate these sort of

14 14:54:51   cracks.  If the waterproof membrane itself fails because

15 14:54:54   it can't breach the cracks, that means water will then

16 14:54:57   get through the membrane and the secondary podium slab

17 14:55:00   and then accumulate on top of the main podium slab,

18 14:55:02   which is about a metre or so below.

19 14:55:07       Slide G3.9-38, please.

20 14:55:12       The design also did not specify any water stops at

21 14:55:15   the construction joint -- for the construction joint and

22 14:55:20   the expansion joints.  There are too many joints there.

23 14:55:23       Basically, at the expansion joints you have the

24 14:55:25   podium slab with an upstand on either side of the joint.

25 14:55:30   Where the upstand connected to the slab, there were no


                                     121
 1 14:55:34   water stops specified in the design.  What that means is

 2 14:55:37   that any water that gets through the waterproof membrane

 3 14:55:40   and then accumulates on top of the main podium slab can

 4 14:55:45   pass through that interface because there will be

 5 14:55:47   a crack there, a construction joint, in effect.

 6 14:55:50       Then, as I showed to you in the images earlier, the

 7 14:55:53   soil in the planters have been placed almost to the

 8 14:55:56   brim, and this means that any surface water will overtop

 9 14:56:00   the waterproof membrane and pass into the podium

10 14:56:03   build-up.

12 14:56:08   are located close to the planter walls, which again will

                                     195
 1 17:11:00   conjunction with the tension piles, which I would expect

23 17:15:16ROF ABDEL WAHAB:  Okay.  And my understanding of these

24 17:15:19   resin injections is that you inject actually the cracks

25 17:15:21   in the concrete to seal them up so that the water would


                                     199
 1 17:15:24   not find a way to come in, in a way.

 2 17:15:29R DE SILVA:  On this one -- so when you say cracks, you can

 3 17:15:32   get structural cracks because of the forces that's

 4 17:15:35   applied on the structure.  But you also have

 5 17:15:37   construction joints, which could be labelled a crack,

 6 17:15:40   but it's not strictly a crack.

 7 17:15:42       So on this particular project, there were resin

 8 17:15:44   injection hoses along the construction joints, at least

 9 17:15:47   in the basement slab, and then there were also resin

10 17:15:51   injection flanges and hoses provided to seal each

11 17:15:56   compartment of the membrane.  So the idea is that you

12 17:15:58   have a compartment that's bounded by the water bars and

13 17:16:01   if you had a puncture in the membrane in the middle of

14 17:16:04   that compartment and you said -- and let's -- for

15 17:16:06   argument's sake, that compartment fills with water

16 17:16:09   between the infrastructure and the membrane, because you

17 17:16:11   have these flanges, the water then enters the flanges

18 17:16:14   through the resin injection hoses and comes out through

19 17:16:16   the boxes.  So by then injecting that particular box,

20 17:16:22   you're filling in that compartment between the membrane

21 17:16:25   and the slab with resin and in effect sealing the

22 17:16:30   puncture in the membrane.
